#### What this PR does / why we need it?


<!--
Use `Fixes #123` for an issue addressed by this PR; it will be closed when the PR merges.
Use `Related:` or `Part of:` to reference an issue without closing it.
-->

Adds KEP-5273, which proposes an opt-in mode where preemption of Pod-integration Workloads honors PodDisruptionBudgets.

Today, when a Workload is preempted, Kueue deletes its Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets are never consulted. For serving workloads that share a cohort with batch workloads, preemption can take down more replicas at once than the application tolerates.

The KEP proposes:

- A `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` feature gate (alpha) and an  `Integrations.PodPreemption` config with `stopPolicy: Delete | Evict` and  `evictionTimeout` (default 10m).
- With `Evict`, Pods of a Workload evicted by preemption are stopped through  the eviction subresource. Other stop reasons (Workload deleted or  deactivated, node lost, PodsReady timeout) still delete.
- A refused eviction sets a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and  is retried with backoff. The Workload keeps its quota until its Pods are  gone, so this never over-admits.
- After `evictionTimeout`, the Pods are deleted as today. A PDB can delay  preemption, but never block it.
- While a victim is blocked, the scheduler can pick other victims whose PDBs  allow the disruption and withdraw the blocked victim's preemption, instead  of waiting on it.

Part of #5273

#### Useful notes for your reviewer

- Earlier discussion on #5273 raised the concern that a strict PDB mode would  let PDBs override quota and priority. This KEP is best effort by design:  the timeout bounds any delay, and the fallback is the current behavior.
- Scope is the Pod integration only. Other integrations keep deleting. I'd  like feedback on whether to widen it in this KEP or a follow-up.
- Open questions in the KEP: the default timeout, how withdrawal interacts  with `CandidatesOrdering`, and handling of partially stopped pod groups.
- A prototype of the pod controller part (eviction, retry, timeout; no  scheduler changes yet) is at   https://github.com/kckang1103/kueue/tree/kep-5273-pdb-aware-preemption.
  It's for reference only, not for review.

## AI summary

This change adds design documentation only. It adds two files under `keps/5273-pdb-aware-preemption/`, for a total of 726 lines added and 0 removed:

- `README.md` (687 lines): Proposes opt-in, PodDisruptionBudget-aware preemption for Pod-integration Workloads. The behavior is gated by the `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` feature gate and `stopPolicy: Evict`. When the API server returns HTTP 429 with a `DisruptionBudget` cause, Kueue sets `DisruptionBlocked=True` with reason `EvictionRefused` and retries until `evictionTimeout`. Other errors, such as overlapping PDBs, are treated as persistent failures. They set `DisruptionBlocked=True` with reason `EvictionFailed` and also retry until the timeout. Transient errors (429 without that cause, 503, server timeout, conflict) return to the reconciler and do not start the timeout. The `evictionTimeout` field defaults to 10 minutes. After the timeout, Kueue deletes the Pods. The document also covers scheduler victim selection, withdrawal of blocked preemptions, configuration validation, metrics, testing, and graduation criteria.
- `kep.yaml` (39 lines): Marks the KEP as provisional and alpha, targets v0.22, names the feature gate, and lists four metrics: `kueue_pod_eviction_refused_total`, `kueue_preemption_eviction_timeouts_total`, `kueue_preemption_withdrawn_total`, and `kueue_preemption_stop_duration_seconds`. The README also mentions `kueue_preemption_withdrawal_races_total`.

The PR does not change Go code, API types, or runtime behavior. The README describes the planned API and behavior.

### Suggested release note

```text
NONE
```

Rationale: The PR adds only KEP documentation, so users see no behavior change. A user-facing release note would apply after the implementation lands. That note would use the `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` feature gate and a `Pod:` or `Scheduling:` prefix.

The proposal could let a PodDisruptionBudget delay preemption indefinitely when two candidate victims are both blocked, which contradicts its own goal of a bounded delay. The withdrawal race bound and the deletion deadline also need to be tightened. Resolve these design points before approving the KEP.

```diff
@@ -0,0 +1,443 @@
+# KEP-5273: Respect PodDisruptionBudgets when preempting Pod-integration Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort](#story-1-serving-tenants-sharing-a-cohort)
+    - [Story 2: a borrower with no PDB](#story-2-a-borrower-with-no-pdb)
+    - [Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up](#story-3-a-victim-whose-budget-is-used-up)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Today's behavior](#todays-behavior)
+  - [Overview](#overview)
+  - [API](#api)
+    - [Configuration](#configuration)
+    - [Workload condition](#workload-condition)
+  - [Pod integration: evict instead of delete](#pod-integration-evict-instead-of-delete)
+  - [Retrying a refused eviction](#retrying-a-refused-eviction)
+  - [Timeout and fallback to deletion](#timeout-and-fallback-to-deletion)
+  - [Scheduler: re-selecting victims](#scheduler-re-selecting-victims)
+  - [Pod groups](#pod-groups)
+  - [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features)
+  - [Metrics](#metrics)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
```

```diff
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)

+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute
+  budgets itself in alpha (see [Alternatives](#alternatives)).
+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.
+- A Pod's quota is still released only when the Pod stops being active
+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota
+  reserved, so a refusal never leads to over-admission.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |
+| Ping-pong: a withdrawn victim is re-selected every cycle. | Candidates that were blocked within the last `evictionTimeout` are ordered after other candidates of the same priority. |
+| Slower reclaim when every candidate is blocked. | Bounded by `evictionTimeout`; the default (10 minutes) is chosen to cover a typical rolling restart, and is configurable. |
+| More API calls (retries). | Retries use exponential backoff capped at 30 seconds per Workload. |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Today's behavior
+
+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption
+   ongoing" and counted as successfully preempted.
+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls
+   `Stop` with reason `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`.
+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the
+   `TerminationTarget` Pod condition and deletes the Pod.
+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once
+   `job.IsActive()` is false.
+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders
+   already-evicted Workloads first, so a victim that never stops keeps being
+   re-selected and the preemptor waits.
```

```diff
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute
+  budgets itself in alpha (see [Alternatives](#alternatives)).
+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.
+- A Pod's quota is still released only when the Pod stops being active
+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota
+  reserved, so a refusal never leads to over-admission.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |
+| Ping-pong: a withdrawn victim is re-selected every cycle. | Candidates that were blocked within the last `evictionTimeout` are ordered after other candidates of the same priority. |
+| Slower reclaim when every candidate is blocked. | Bounded by `evictionTimeout`; the default (10 minutes) is chosen to cover a typical rolling restart, and is configurable. |
+| More API calls (retries). | Retries use exponential backoff capped at 30 seconds per Workload. |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Today's behavior
+
+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption
+   ongoing" and counted as successfully preempted.
+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls
+   `Stop` with reason `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`.
+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the
+   `TerminationTarget` Pod condition and deletes the Pod.
+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once
+   `job.IsActive()` is false.
+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders
+   already-evicted Workloads first, so a victim that never stops keeps being
+   re-selected and the preemptor waits.
```

+# KEP-5273: Respect PodDisruptionBudgets when preempting Pod-integration Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)

+    - [Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort](#story-1-serving-tenants-sharing-a-cohort)
+    - [Story 2: a borrower with no PDB](#story-2-a-borrower-with-no-pdb)
+    - [Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up](#story-3-a-victim-whose-budget-is-used-up)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Today's behavior](#todays-behavior)
+  - [Overview](#overview)
+  - [API](#api)
+    - [Configuration](#configuration)
+    - [Workload condition](#workload-condition)
+  - [Pod integration: evict instead of delete](#pod-integration-evict-instead-of-delete)
+  - [Retrying a refused eviction](#retrying-a-refused-eviction)
+  - [Timeout and fallback to deletion](#timeout-and-fallback-to-deletion)
+  - [Scheduler: re-selecting victims](#scheduler-re-selecting-victims)
+  - [Pod groups](#pod-groups)
+  - [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features)
+  - [Metrics](#metrics)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute
+  budgets itself in alpha (see [Alternatives](#alternatives)).
+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.
+- A Pod's quota is still released only when the Pod stops being active
+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota
+  reserved, so a refusal never leads to over-admission.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |
+| Ping-pong: a withdrawn victim is re-selected every cycle. | Candidates that were blocked within the last `evictionTimeout` are ordered after other candidates of the same priority. |
+| Slower reclaim when every candidate is blocked. | Bounded by `evictionTimeout`; the default (10 minutes) is chosen to cover a typical rolling restart, and is configurable. |
+| More API calls (retries). | Retries use exponential backoff capped at 30 seconds per Workload. |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Today's behavior
+
+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption
+   ongoing" and counted as successfully preempted.
+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls
+   `Stop` with reason `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`.
+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the
+   `TerminationTarget` Pod condition and deletes the Pod.
+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once
+   `job.IsActive()` is false.
+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders
+   already-evicted Workloads first, so a victim that never stops keeps being
+   re-selected and the preemptor waits.
+
+With deletion, step 5 never stalls. With eviction and no further change it
+would stall for as long as the PDB refuses. The scheduler change below is what
+prevents that.
+
+### Overview
+
+```
+scheduler                         job reconciler (Pod integration)
+---------                         --------------------------------
+Evict(victim, Preempted)  ----->  Stop(): POST pods/eviction
+                                    ok   -> Pod terminates, quota released (as today)
+                                    429  -> set DisruptionBlocked=True, requeue (backoff)
+next cycle:                                 after evictionTimeout -> Delete (as today)
+  victim Evicted && DisruptionBlocked?
+    other victims can cover need ->
+      withdraw victim (clear Evicted), evict others
+    none can -> keep waiting (timeout will delete)
+```
+
+### API
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new optional field in `Configuration.Integrations` (v1beta2):
+
+```go
+type Integrations struct {
+	// ...existing fields...
+
+	// podPreemption configures how the Pod integration stops Pods of a
+	// Workload that was evicted by preemption. Requires the
+	// PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets feature gate.
+	// +optional
+	PodPreemption *PodPreemption `json:"podPreemption,omitempty"`
+}
+
+type PodPreemptionStopPolicy string
+
+const (
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete deletes the Pods. This is the default
+	// and today's behavior.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Delete"
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict stops the Pods through the eviction
+	// subresource, so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored until evictionTimeout.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Evict"
+)
+
+type PodPreemption struct {
+	// stopPolicy is Delete (default) or Evict.
+	// +optional
+	StopPolicy PodPreemptionStopPolicy `json:"stopPolicy,omitempty"`
+
+	// evictionTimeout bounds how long Kueue keeps retrying an eviction
+	// refused by a PodDisruptionBudget before deleting the Pods.
+	// Defaults to 10m. Must be greater than 0.
+	// +optional
+	EvictionTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"evictionTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+A per-ClusterQueue override (`spec.preemption.evictionTimeout`) is deferred to
+beta, pending feedback on whether a global value is enough.
+
+#### Workload condition
+
+A new Workload condition type:
+
+```go
+const (
+	// WorkloadDisruptionBlocked means that stopping the Workload's Pods after
+	// a preemption was refused by a PodDisruptionBudget. LastTransitionTime is
+	// when the refusals started; the message names the refusing budgets.
+	WorkloadDisruptionBlocked = "DisruptionBlocked"
+)
+```
+
+It is set to `True` on the first refused eviction, and removed when the Workload
+stops being evicted (the eviction succeeded and admission was cleared, the
+preemption was withdrawn, or the Pods were deleted after the timeout).
```

@@ -0,0 +1,443 @@
+# KEP-5273: Respect PodDisruptionBudgets when preempting Pod-integration Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort](#story-1-serving-tenants-sharing-a-cohort)
+    - [Story 2: a borrower with no PDB](#story-2-a-borrower-with-no-pdb)
+    - [Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up](#story-3-a-victim-whose-budget-is-used-up)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Today's behavior](#todays-behavior)
+  - [Overview](#overview)
+  - [API](#api)
+    - [Configuration](#configuration)
+    - [Workload condition](#workload-condition)
+  - [Pod integration: evict instead of delete](#pod-integration-evict-instead-of-delete)
+  - [Retrying a refused eviction](#retrying-a-refused-eviction)
+  - [Timeout and fallback to deletion](#timeout-and-fallback-to-deletion)
+  - [Scheduler: re-selecting victims](#scheduler-re-selecting-victims)
+  - [Pod groups](#pod-groups)
+  - [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features)
+  - [Metrics](#metrics)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute
+  budgets itself in alpha (see [Alternatives](#alternatives)).
+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.
+- A Pod's quota is still released only when the Pod stops being active
+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota
+  reserved, so a refusal never leads to over-admission.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |
+| Ping-pong: a withdrawn victim is re-selected every cycle. | Candidates that were blocked within the last `evictionTimeout` are ordered after other candidates of the same priority. |
+| Slower reclaim when every candidate is blocked. | Bounded by `evictionTimeout`; the default (10 minutes) is chosen to cover a typical rolling restart, and is configurable. |
+| More API calls (retries). | Retries use exponential backoff capped at 30 seconds per Workload. |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Today's behavior
+
+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption
+   ongoing" and counted as successfully preempted.
+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls
+   `Stop` with reason `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`.
+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the
+   `TerminationTarget` Pod condition and deletes the Pod.
+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once
+   `job.IsActive()` is false.
+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders
+   already-evicted Workloads first, so a victim that never stops keeps being
+   re-selected and the preemptor waits.
+
+With deletion, step 5 never stalls. With eviction and no further change it
+would stall for as long as the PDB refuses. The scheduler change below is what
+prevents that.
+
+### Overview
+
+```
+scheduler                         job reconciler (Pod integration)
+---------                         --------------------------------
+Evict(victim, Preempted)  ----->  Stop(): POST pods/eviction
+                                    ok   -> Pod terminates, quota released (as today)
+                                    429  -> set DisruptionBlocked=True, requeue (backoff)
+next cycle:                                 after evictionTimeout -> Delete (as today)
+  victim Evicted && DisruptionBlocked?
+    other victims can cover need ->
+      withdraw victim (clear Evicted), evict others
+    none can -> keep waiting (timeout will delete)
+```
+
+### API
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new optional field in `Configuration.Integrations` (v1beta2):
+
+```go
+type Integrations struct {
+	// ...existing fields...
+
+	// podPreemption configures how the Pod integration stops Pods of a
+	// Workload that was evicted by preemption. Requires the
+	// PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets feature gate.
+	// +optional
+	PodPreemption *PodPreemption `json:"podPreemption,omitempty"`
+}
+
+type PodPreemptionStopPolicy string
+
+const (
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete deletes the Pods. This is the default
+	// and today's behavior.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Delete"
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict stops the Pods through the eviction
+	// subresource, so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored until evictionTimeout.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Evict"
+)
+
+type PodPreemption struct {
+	// stopPolicy is Delete (default) or Evict.
+	// +optional
+	StopPolicy PodPreemptionStopPolicy `json:"stopPolicy,omitempty"`
+
+	// evictionTimeout bounds how long Kueue keeps retrying an eviction
+	// refused by a PodDisruptionBudget before deleting the Pods.
+	// Defaults to 10m. Must be greater than 0.
+	// +optional
+	EvictionTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"evictionTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+A per-ClusterQueue override (`spec.preemption.evictionTimeout`) is deferred to
+beta, pending feedback on whether a global value is enough.
+
+#### Workload condition
+
+A new Workload condition type:
+
+```go
+const (
+	// WorkloadDisruptionBlocked means that stopping the Workload's Pods after
+	// a preemption was refused by a PodDisruptionBudget. LastTransitionTime is
+	// when the refusals started; the message names the refusing budgets.
+	WorkloadDisruptionBlocked = "DisruptionBlocked"
+)
+```
+
+It is set to `True` on the first refused eviction, and removed when the Workload
+stops being evicted (the eviction succeeded and admission was cleared, the
+preemption was withdrawn, or the Pods were deleted after the timeout).
```

+
+## Summary

+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on

+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim

+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.

+## Motivation

+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block

+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout

+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction

+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.

+  Pods.

+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.

+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.

+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.

+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute

+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.

+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota

+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |

+

+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption

+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls

+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the

+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once

+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders

+would stall for as long as the PDB refuses. The scheduler change below is what
+prevents that.
+
+### Overview
+
+```
+scheduler                         job reconciler (Pod integration)
+---------                         --------------------------------
+Evict(victim, Preempted)  ----->  Stop(): POST pods/eviction
+                                    ok   -> Pod terminates, quota released (as today)
+                                    429  -> set DisruptionBlocked=True, requeue (backoff)
+next cycle:                                 after evictionTimeout -> Delete (as today)
+  victim Evicted && DisruptionBlocked?
+    other victims can cover need ->
+      withdraw victim (clear Evicted), evict others
+    none can -> keep waiting (timeout will delete)
+```
+
+### API
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new optional field in `Configuration.Integrations` (v1beta2):
+
+```go
+type Integrations struct {
+	// ...existing fields...
+
+	// podPreemption configures how the Pod integration stops Pods of a
+	// Workload that was evicted by preemption. Requires the
+	// PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets feature gate.
+	// +optional
+	PodPreemption *PodPreemption `json:"podPreemption,omitempty"`
+}
+
+type PodPreemptionStopPolicy string
+
+const (
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete deletes the Pods. This is the default
+	// and today's behavior.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Delete"
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict stops the Pods through the eviction
+	// subresource, so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored until evictionTimeout.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Evict"
+)
+
+type PodPreemption struct {
+	// stopPolicy is Delete (default) or Evict.
+	// +optional
+	StopPolicy PodPreemptionStopPolicy `json:"stopPolicy,omitempty"`
+
+	// evictionTimeout bounds how long Kueue keeps retrying an eviction
+	// refused by a PodDisruptionBudget before deleting the Pods.
+	// Defaults to 10m. Must be greater than 0.
+	// +optional
+	EvictionTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"evictionTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+A per-ClusterQueue override (`spec.preemption.evictionTimeout`) is deferred to
+beta, pending feedback on whether a global value is enough.
+
+#### Workload condition
+
+A new Workload condition type:
+
+```go
+const (
+	// WorkloadDisruptionBlocked means that stopping the Workload's Pods after
+	// a preemption was refused by a PodDisruptionBudget. LastTransitionTime is
+	// when the refusals started; the message names the refusing budgets.
+	WorkloadDisruptionBlocked = "DisruptionBlocked"
+)
+```
+
+It is set to `True` on the first refused eviction, and removed when the Workload
+stops being evicted (the eviction succeeded and admission was cleared, the
+preemption was withdrawn, or the Pods were deleted after the timeout).
+
+### Pod integration: evict instead of delete
+
+In `Pod.Stop`, when the gate is enabled, the policy is `Evict`, and the stop
+reason is `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`:
+
+```go
+// Delete the pod if it is not already deleted.
+if err == nil {
+	if p.shouldEvict(stopReason) && !p.evictionTimedOut(wl) {
+		err := c.SubResource("eviction").Create(ctx, podInGroup.Object(),
+			&policyv1.Eviction{ObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{
+				Name: podInGroup.pod.Name, Namespace: podInGroup.pod.Namespace}})
+		switch {
+		case apierrors.IsTooManyRequests(err):
+			blocked = append(blocked, podInGroup.Object())
+			continue // not stopped; reported to the reconciler below
+		case client.IgnoreNotFound(err) != nil:
+			return stoppedNow, err
+		}
```

  <!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->
  
  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/major-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Major** · **Functional Correctness** · **Quick win**
  
  **Handle eviction errors other than a PDB 429 so the timeout always applies.**
  
  Only `IsTooManyRequests` leads to `DisruptionBlocked` and the timeout. This has two gaps:
  - The API server returns 429 for API throttling too, not only for a PDB refusal. Such a 429 is then recorded as a PDB block and can lead to withdrawing a healthy victim.
  - A Pod matched by several PDBs gets an error that is not 429. It reaches `return stoppedNow, err`, so the reconciler retries on error and never reaches `evictionTimedOut`. A misconfigured PDB then prevents preemption, which breaks the goal that a PDB can never block a preemption indefinitely.
  
  Decide how both cases reach the timeout. Distinguish a PDB refusal by its status cause or message. Count other persistent eviction errors against `evictionTimeout` too.

@@ -0,0 +1,443 @@
+# KEP-5273: Respect PodDisruptionBudgets when preempting Pod-integration Workloads
+
+<!-- toc -->
+- [Summary](#summary)
+- [Motivation](#motivation)
+  - [Goals](#goals)
+  - [Non-Goals](#non-goals)
+- [Proposal](#proposal)
+  - [User Stories](#user-stories)
+    - [Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort](#story-1-serving-tenants-sharing-a-cohort)
+    - [Story 2: a borrower with no PDB](#story-2-a-borrower-with-no-pdb)
+    - [Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up](#story-3-a-victim-whose-budget-is-used-up)
+  - [Notes/Constraints/Caveats](#notesconstraintscaveats)
+  - [Risks and Mitigations](#risks-and-mitigations)
+- [Design Details](#design-details)
+  - [Today's behavior](#todays-behavior)
+  - [Overview](#overview)
+  - [API](#api)
+    - [Configuration](#configuration)
+    - [Workload condition](#workload-condition)
+  - [Pod integration: evict instead of delete](#pod-integration-evict-instead-of-delete)
+  - [Retrying a refused eviction](#retrying-a-refused-eviction)
+  - [Timeout and fallback to deletion](#timeout-and-fallback-to-deletion)
+  - [Scheduler: re-selecting victims](#scheduler-re-selecting-victims)
+  - [Pod groups](#pod-groups)
+  - [Interaction with other features](#interaction-with-other-features)
+  - [Metrics](#metrics)
+  - [Test Plan](#test-plan)
+    - [Unit tests](#unit-tests)
+    - [Integration tests](#integration-tests)
+    - [e2e tests](#e2e-tests)
+  - [Graduation Criteria](#graduation-criteria)
+- [Implementation History](#implementation-history)
+- [Drawbacks](#drawbacks)
+- [Alternatives](#alternatives)
+<!-- /toc -->
+
+## Summary
+
+When Kueue preempts a Workload managed by the Pod integration (plain Pods, pod
+groups, and the Deployment, StatefulSet and LeaderWorkerSet integrations built on
+it), it deletes the Pods directly. PodDisruptionBudgets (PDBs) are never
+consulted.
+
+This KEP adds an opt-in mode in which the Pod integration stops a preempted
+Workload through the Pod `eviction` subresource, so PDBs are honored on a
+best-effort basis:
+
+1. A refused eviction is retried.
+2. While a victim's eviction is refused, the scheduler may pick another victim
+   whose budget allows the disruption, and withdraw the preemption of the
+   blocked one.
+3. A configurable timeout bounds how long any eviction can be refused. After it
+   expires, Kueue deletes the Pods as it does today, so a PDB can delay a
+   preemption but can never prevent it.
+
+## Motivation
+
+Kueue's preemption is increasingly used for long-running serving workloads, not
+only batch. A common setup is a cohort shared by several tenants: each
+ClusterQueue has a guaranteed nominal quota, lends what it does not use, and
+reclaims it with `reclaimWithinCohort` when its own demand returns. Serving
+tenants express their disruption tolerance with PDBs, and every other voluntary
+disruption in the cluster (node drains, upgrades, the cluster autoscaler)
+respects them. Kueue preemption is the exception: it can take a Deployment below
+its declared `minAvailable` in one scheduling cycle.
+
+Issue [#5273](https://github.com/kubernetes-sigs/kueue/issues/5273) asks for PDBs
+to be respected. The concern raised there is valid: if a PDB could block
+preemption outright, a tenant could keep borrowed quota forever, or a
+low-priority Workload could block a high-priority one, just by writing a strict
+PDB. Quota and priority are set by the administrator; PDBs are written by the
+tenant being preempted, and must not be able to override the former.
+
+This proposal resolves that tension the same way kube-scheduler does: PDBs shape
+which victims are chosen and how fast they are disrupted, and a bounded timeout
+guarantees that preemption always completes.
+
+### Goals
+
+- Stop Pod-integration Workloads evicted by preemption through the Pod eviction
+  subresource, so PDBs are honored, behind a feature gate and a configuration
+  option.
+- Never let a PDB block a preemption indefinitely: bound every refusal with a
+  configurable timeout, then delete as today.
+- When a victim's PDB refuses, let the scheduler preempt a different eligible
+  victim instead of waiting, so honoring PDBs does not slow reclaim when other
+  victims can be disrupted.
+- Keep today's behavior unchanged when the feature is disabled.
+
+### Non-Goals
+
+- Job-based integrations (batch/Job, JobSet, RayJob, Kubeflow jobs, and so on).
+  Kueue stops those by suspending the job, and their own controllers remove the
+  Pods.
+- Evictions for reasons other than preemption (deactivation, PodsReady timeout,
+  admission checks, a stopped ClusterQueue, node failures, Workload deletion).
+  These keep deleting Pods.
+- Strict PDB enforcement, where a PDB can prevent a preemption forever.
+- Topology- or node-aware victim selection.
+
+## Proposal
+
+Add a feature gate `PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets` (alpha, disabled by
+default) and a configuration option to choose how the Pod integration stops a
+preempted Workload: `Delete` (today's behavior and the default) or `Evict`.
+
+With `Evict`:
+
+1. The Pod integration calls the eviction subresource instead of deleting.
+2. If the API server refuses (HTTP 429, a PDB has no disruptions left), Kueue
+   records a `DisruptionBlocked` condition on the Workload and retries with
+   backoff.
+3. On its next cycle, the scheduler treats a blocked victim as not progressing.
+   If other eligible victims can cover the preemptor's need, it preempts them and
+   withdraws the preemption of the blocked victim, which keeps its admission.
+4. If a victim stays blocked for longer than `evictionTimeout`, Kueue deletes its
+   Pods, as today.
+
+### User Stories
+
+#### Story 1: serving tenants sharing a cohort
+
+Two serving tenants share a cohort. Tenant A lends idle capacity overnight;
+tenant B borrows it. In the morning A's Deployments scale up and reclaim. B runs
+many Deployments, each with a PDB allowing one or two Pods to be unavailable.
+Today Kueue deletes as many of B's Pods as A needs at once, possibly most of one
+Deployment. With this KEP, B gives back the same capacity spread across its
+Deployments, each within its budget, and A is not delayed because the scheduler
+moves to the next victim whenever a budget refuses.
+
+#### Story 2: a borrower with no PDB
+
+A training tenant borrows idle capacity and has no PDBs. The eviction subresource
+behaves like deletion for Pods not covered by a PDB, so preemption of this tenant
+is unchanged.
+
+#### Story 3: a victim whose budget is used up
+
+Every candidate's PDB currently has no disruptions left, for example because
+several replicas are already unready. Kueue retries for `evictionTimeout`, then
+deletes, as today. The preemptor waited at most `evictionTimeout`.
+
+### Notes/Constraints/Caveats
+
+- PDBs are evaluated by the API server at eviction time. Kueue does not compute
+  budgets itself in alpha (see [Alternatives](#alternatives)).
+- Only the `Preempted` eviction reason uses the eviction subresource. A Workload
+  evicted for any other reason, or deleted, keeps today's deletion path.
+- A Pod's quota is still released only when the Pod stops being active
+  (`Pod.IsActive`). A refused eviction leaves the Pod running and its quota
+  reserved, so a refusal never leads to over-admission.
+
+### Risks and Mitigations
+
+| Risk | Mitigation |
+|---|---|
+| A tenant uses a strict PDB to keep borrowed quota. | `evictionTimeout` always applies; a refusal can only delay preemption. |
+| Over-preemption: the scheduler picks a replacement victim, then the blocked victim's eviction succeeds anyway. | The blocked victim's preemption is withdrawn (Evicted cleared) before or together with issuing the replacement, so the reconciler stops retrying it. The remaining race (a retry in flight when the withdrawal lands) can disrupt at most one extra Pod per withdrawn victim; documented, and counted by a metric. |
+| Ping-pong: a withdrawn victim is re-selected every cycle. | Candidates that were blocked within the last `evictionTimeout` are ordered after other candidates of the same priority. |
+| Slower reclaim when every candidate is blocked. | Bounded by `evictionTimeout`; the default (10 minutes) is chosen to cover a typical rolling restart, and is configurable. |
+| More API calls (retries). | Retries use exponential backoff capped at 30 seconds per Workload. |
+
+## Design Details
+
+### Today's behavior
+
+1. `Preemptor.IssuePreemptions` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/preemption.go`) calls
+   `workloadevict.Evict`, which sets the `Evicted` condition with reason
+   `Preempted`. Victims already marked `Evicted` are reported as "Preemption
+   ongoing" and counted as successfully preempted.
+2. The job reconciler (`pkg/controller/jobframework/reconciler.go`, step 6,
+   "handle eviction") calls `stopJob`, which for a `ComposableJob` calls
+   `Stop` with reason `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`.
+3. `Pod.Stop` (`pkg/controller/jobs/pod/pod_controller.go`) sets the
+   `TerminationTarget` Pod condition and deletes the Pod.
+4. The reconciler clears the Workload's quota reservation once
+   `job.IsActive()` is false.
+5. `CandidatesOrdering` (`pkg/scheduler/preemption/common/ordering.go`) orders
+   already-evicted Workloads first, so a victim that never stops keeps being
+   re-selected and the preemptor waits.
+
+With deletion, step 5 never stalls. With eviction and no further change it
+would stall for as long as the PDB refuses. The scheduler change below is what
+prevents that.
+
+### Overview
+
+```
+scheduler                         job reconciler (Pod integration)
+---------                         --------------------------------
+Evict(victim, Preempted)  ----->  Stop(): POST pods/eviction
+                                    ok   -> Pod terminates, quota released (as today)
+                                    429  -> set DisruptionBlocked=True, requeue (backoff)
+next cycle:                                 after evictionTimeout -> Delete (as today)
+  victim Evicted && DisruptionBlocked?
+    other victims can cover need ->
+      withdraw victim (clear Evicted), evict others
+    none can -> keep waiting (timeout will delete)
+```
+
+### API
+
+#### Configuration
+
+A new optional field in `Configuration.Integrations` (v1beta2):
+
+```go
+type Integrations struct {
+	// ...existing fields...
+
+	// podPreemption configures how the Pod integration stops Pods of a
+	// Workload that was evicted by preemption. Requires the
+	// PreemptionRespectsPodDisruptionBudgets feature gate.
+	// +optional
+	PodPreemption *PodPreemption `json:"podPreemption,omitempty"`
+}
+
+type PodPreemptionStopPolicy string
+
+const (
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete deletes the Pods. This is the default
+	// and today's behavior.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyDelete PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Delete"
+	// PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict stops the Pods through the eviction
+	// subresource, so PodDisruptionBudgets are honored until evictionTimeout.
+	PodPreemptionStopPolicyEvict PodPreemptionStopPolicy = "Evict"
+)
+
+type PodPreemption struct {
+	// stopPolicy is Delete (default) or Evict.
+	// +optional
+	StopPolicy PodPreemptionStopPolicy `json:"stopPolicy,omitempty"`
+
+	// evictionTimeout bounds how long Kueue keeps retrying an eviction
+	// refused by a PodDisruptionBudget before deleting the Pods.
+	// Defaults to 10m. Must be greater than 0.
+	// +optional
+	EvictionTimeout *metav1.Duration `json:"evictionTimeout,omitempty"`
+}
+```
+
+A per-ClusterQueue override (`spec.preemption.evictionTimeout`) is deferred to
+beta, pending feedback on whether a global value is enough.
+
+#### Workload condition
+
+A new Workload condition type:
+
+```go
+const (
+	// WorkloadDisruptionBlocked means that stopping the Workload's Pods after
+	// a preemption was refused by a PodDisruptionBudget. LastTransitionTime is
+	// when the refusals started; the message names the refusing budgets.
+	WorkloadDisruptionBlocked = "DisruptionBlocked"
+)
+```
+
+It is set to `True` on the first refused eviction, and removed when the Workload
+stops being evicted (the eviction succeeded and admission was cleared, the
+preemption was withdrawn, or the Pods were deleted after the timeout).
+
+### Pod integration: evict instead of delete
+
+In `Pod.Stop`, when the gate is enabled, the policy is `Evict`, and the stop
+reason is `WorkloadEvictedDueToPreempted`:
+
+```go
+// Delete the pod if it is not already deleted.
+if err == nil {
+	if p.shouldEvict(stopReason) && !p.evictionTimedOut(wl) {
+		err := c.SubResource("eviction").Create(ctx, podInGroup.Object(),
+			&policyv1.Eviction{ObjectMeta: metav1.ObjectMeta{
+				Name: podInGroup.pod.Name, Namespace: podInGroup.pod.Namespace}})
+		switch {
+		case apierrors.IsTooManyRequests(err):
+			blocked = append(blocked, podInGroup.Object())
+			continue // not stopped; reported to the reconciler below
+		case client.IgnoreNotFound(err) != nil:
+			return stoppedNow, err
+		}
+	} else if err := c.Delete(ctx, podInGroup.Object()); client.IgnoreNotFound(err) != nil {
+		return stoppedNow, err
+	}
+	// ...finalizer handling unchanged...
+}
+```
+
+The `TerminationTarget` condition is still applied before the eviction, as
+today.
+
+### Retrying a refused eviction
+
+`Stop` reports refused Pods to the reconciler through a typed error
+(`jobframework.ErrDisruptionBlocked`, carrying the refused Pods and the PDB
+names from the 429 response). On that error, the reconciler's eviction step:
+
+1. sets `DisruptionBlocked=True` on the Workload (only on the first refusal, so
+   `LastTransitionTime` marks when refusals started),
+2. returns `ctrl.Result{RequeueAfter: backoff}`, with backoff starting at 1
+   second and doubling to a cap of 30 seconds,
```

  <!-- coderabbit-ai-generated:v1 -->
  
  **<picture><img src="https://storage.googleapis.com/coderabbit_public_assets/review-comment-icons-v1/amber-16.svg" width="16" height="16" align="absmiddle" alt=""></picture> Minor** · **Functional Correctness** · **Quick win**
  
  **State where the retry backoff is kept.**
  
  `ctrl.Result{RequeueAfter: backoff}` keeps no memory between reconciles. A backoff that starts at 1 second and doubles to 30 seconds needs an attempt count or the last refusal time. The KEP does not say where it lives, for example a rate-limited workqueue, in-memory state or the condition. Without state, every retry uses the same delay. After a restart of kueue-controller-manager, the backoff resets, so name the chosen source.
